People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Hi... well, I guess I came in because I just can’t stop feeling like everything that goes wrong is my fault. Like I’m a terrible employee, and a really bad homeowner, and I just deserve to lose everything I’ve worked for. 

I mean, I work in business operations, so I’m supposed to keep things running smoothly, right? But the other day, I was looking at my house and the gutters were completely clogged. I bought this ladder from Home Depot last month—it was one of those telescoping ones, they had a really good sale going on in the garden center. I actually went in there just to buy some potting soil for my ferns. Ferns are just so hard to keep alive indoors, the air gets way too dry in the winter. I tried misting them with a spray bottle, but it leaves these little water spots on the hardwood floors, and then you have to use that special wood cleaner, the orange-scented kind...

Um, as a kid? Easily distracted... I don't really remember anyone saying that specifically. I mean, I got decent grades, mostly B's, so I guess I paid attention enough. 

My fourth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable, she used to give us these little gold star stickers if we finished our reading assignments on time. I loved those stickers. I actually saw some similar ones at Michael's the other day when I was looking for new pens for my planner. Michael's always has those 40% off coupons, but you have to download their app to use them now. It's so annoying how every single store wants you to download their app. My phone storage is completely full because of it. I keep getting that pop-up saying 'Storage Almost Full,' and it won't even let me take a picture without deleting something first. I really need to back up my photos to my laptop, but I lost the little dongle thing that connects the USB to the newer MacBooks. Apple changing all their ports was such a money grab, honestly. My friend Sarah bought a knock-off adapter on Amazon and it completely fried her hard drive. She lost all her college photos, which is so sad because she studied abroad in Italy and had all these amazing pictures of the Colosseum and...

Oh, gosh. I'm sorry. I'm doing it again. What did you ask me? About being a kid? But yeah, no, I don't think I was unusually distracted back then.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, worrying excessively? I mean, I guess I worry a normal amount about work and stuff, but not excessively. I definitely check things a lot, but I wouldn't say I'm sitting around worrying about my health or finances all day. 

Although, speaking of finances, I did just have to pay my car insurance renewal, and it went up by like thirty dollars a month for absolutely no reason. I haven't even had a ticket or an accident or anything! I tried calling my agent, but he was out of the office for a golf tournament. It's crazy how much business gets done on golf courses, honestly. I bought this starter set of clubs from a guy on Facebook Marketplace, but the bag smelled like old cigarettes. It's so hard to get smoke smell out of fabric. Wait, sorry. I completely lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? About worrying? Yeah, no, I don't think I worry about all those different things excessively. Just the normal amount, I guess.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, compelled to continue? I don't know if it's about it feeling "just right," but... yeah, there are times I realize I’ve been pacing the same path from the kitchen to the front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers and checking the peephole over and over, even though nothing has actually happened. 

It's just, the layout of my apartment makes it really easy to pace that exact route. It's an open-concept floor plan, which everyone says they want on those HGTV shows. Have you ever watched House Hunters? It's wild to me how people will have a budget of like, two million dollars, and their job is something like "part-time hamster groomer." But anyway, open concept is great for light, but terrible for cooking smells. Whenever I make salmon, the whole place smells like fish for two days. I tried lighting one of those expensive Bath & Body Works candles—the mahogany teakwood one, which smells amazing, kind of like men's cologne?—but it barely made a dent. I think you're supposed to simmer lemons and rosemary on the stove to really get rid of cooking odors. My grandma used to do that. She had this huge rosemary bush in her backyard that practically took over the whole fence. Rosemary is so resilient, you barely even have to water it. I tried growing a basil plant on my windowsill once, but I forgot to water it for three days and it completely shriveled up. I just don't have a green thumb at all, which is sad because I'd love to have one of those trendy fiddle leaf figs...

Wait, I'm doing it again, aren't I? I'm so sorry. What did you ask me? About the peephole? But yeah, I just... I do keep pacing and checking it, and rubbing my fingers together, even when I know nobody is there.

What was the question? Oh, feeling down. Um, no, I haven't really noticed feeling sad or empty most of the day.\n\nDoctor: When you were a child, did teachers or family often describe you as easily distracted?\n\nPatient: Um, as a kid? Easily distracted... I don't really remember anyone saying that specifically. I mean, I got decent grades, mostly B's, so I guess I paid attention enough. \n\nMy fourth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable, she used to give us these little gold star stickers if we finished our reading assignments on time. I loved those stickers. I actually saw some similar ones at Michael's the other day when I was looking for new pens for my planner. Um, how much time? I don't know exactly. It's hard to keep track of time when I get like that. But like I said before, I just realize I've been pacing that same path, kitchen to front door, rubbing my thumb on my fingers, checking the peephole over and over, even when nothing's happened. 

It feels like it can go on for a while, though. Time is so weird lately anyway. Have you noticed how fast this year is going? It's already almost October. I walked into Target yesterday to get paper towels, and they already had all the Christmas stuff out. It's ridiculous. They haven't even gotten through Halloween yet! I love Halloween, mostly for the candy. Those little fun-size Snickers bars are my weakness. I try to buy them at the last minute so I don't eat the whole bag before the trick-or-treaters come. Last year, I bought three bags of Reese's peanut butter cups in September, and I had to go back and buy more the day before Halloween because I ate them all while watching Netflix. I was watching that baking show, the Great British Baking Show. It's so soothing, you know? The music is so calm, and everyone is so polite to each other, even when their cakes collapse. I tried baking a cake once, a lemon drizzle one like they make on the show, but I forgot the baking powder and it came out like a dense, lemony brick. My dog wouldn't even eat it, and he eats everything. He ate a whole tube of chapstick once and was totally fine. It was the cherry flavored kind, too...

Wait, sorry. I'm doing it again. What did you ask? Oh, how much time I spend doing the pacing and checking? I guess... maybe a few hours a day? It just kind of takes over, and I don't realize how long I've been doing it.

Um, unable to resist the urge? I don't know if it's an urge, really. It's more like... I just find myself doing it. Like I mentioned, I'll just suddenly realize I've been pacing that same exact path, you know, kitchen to front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers, and looking through the peephole over and over, even when absolutely nothing has happened out there. 

It's just so automatic. Kind of like driving to work, you know? Have you ever had that happen where you drive your whole commute and pull into the parking lot, and you don't even remember the drive? Highway hypnosis, I think they call it. My commute is awful, honestly. The traffic on I-95 is a nightmare no matter what time you leave. I tried leaving at 6:30 AM once to beat the rush, and there was still a bumper-to-bumper backup because a truck spilled a load of gravel across three lanes. I ended up listening to an entire true crime podcast episode while just sitting there. I love true crime podcasts, but sometimes they freak me out, especially the ones about unsolved mysteries. I always end up double-checking all my locks after I listen to them. I even bought one of those extra security bars for my sliding glass door, the kind you wedge into the track? My dad told me to get one when I moved into this apartment. He's always been really big on home security. He has cameras set up all over his house that send alerts to his phone every time a squirrel runs across the porch. He showed me the app once, and it was just dozens of notifications of a fat squirrel stealing birdseed...

Oh, gosh, I'm doing it again. I'm sorry. The urge to stop? Yeah, I don't know if it's an urge I can't resist, it's just... I realize I'm already doing it, pacing and checking and rubbing my fingers, before I even think about stopping.

Um, how do I feel if I stop before I'm finished? I don't really know if there is a "finished" feeling, honestly. Like I was saying a minute ago, there are just times I realize I’ve been pacing that same exact path from the kitchen to the front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers and checking the peephole over and over. And nothing has actually happened out there to make me do it. So if I try to just force myself to sit down, I guess I just feel kind of... jittery? 

Like I have all this nervous energy, kind of like when you drink way too much caffeine. Our office actually just got one of those fancy new espresso machines in the breakroom. It's supposed to be this great employee perk, but honestly, the beans they buy for it are so acidic. It gives me terrible heartburn. I had to start keeping a giant bottle of Tums in my bottom desk drawer. The berry flavored ones aren't too bad, but the chalky texture is just gross. It totally reminds me of those candy cigarettes we used to buy from the ice cream truck when I was a kid. Can you believe they actually sold those to children? We used to pretend we were smoking them behind the bleachers at recess. The ice cream man also had those SpongeBob popsicles where the gumball eyes were always melted and terrifying looking. I always wanted the Choco Taco, though. I was so sad when they discontinued those recently. I saw a petition online to bring them back, and it had like a hundred thousand signatures. People get really passionate about nostalgic snacks...

Oh, gosh, sorry. I went off again. What were we talking about? The pacing? Right, yeah. I don't really feel a specific way if I stop, because I don't really decide to start or stop. I just suddenly realize I've been doing the whole kitchen-to-door pacing and checking thing over and over for no reason.

I tried getting into bullet journaling last year because I saw all these amazing, aesthetic spreads on Pinterest. Pinterest is so addictive, honestly. I spent like three hours the other night just pinning recipes for overnight oats. Oats are supposedly really good for your cholesterol, which I guess I shouldn't worry about too much at 27, but my dad just had to go on statins and he was complaining about the side effects. He said it makes his muscles ache, especially when he walks the dog. They have a golden retriever, she's so cute but she sheds absolutely everywhere. You really need a good vacuum for pet hair, like one of those cordless Dysons. Dysons are so expensive though, you really have to wait for the Black Friday sales if you want to get a decent deal on one... \n\nWait, I'm so sorry, I totally lost track again. What was the question? Oh, feeling down. Um, no, I haven't really noticed feeling sad or empty most of the day.\n\nDoctor: When you were a child, did teachers or family often describe you as easily distracted?\n\nPatient: Um, as a kid? Easily distracted... I don't really remember anyone saying that specifically. I mean, I got decent grades, mostly B's, so I guess I paid attention enough. \n\nMy fourth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable, she used to give us these little gold star stickers if we finished our reading assignments on time. I loved those stickers. I actually saw some similar ones at Michael's the other day when I was looking for new pens for my planner. Michael's always has those 40% off coupons, but you have to download their app to use them now. It's so annoying how every single store wants you to download their app. My phone storage is completely full because of it. I keep getting that pop-up saying 'Storage Almost Full,' and it won't even let me take a picture without deleting something first. I really need to back up my photos to my laptop, but I lost the little dongle thing that connects the USB to the newer MacBooks. Apple changing all their ports was such a money grab, honestly. My friend Sarah bought a knock-off adapter on Amazon and it completely fried her hard drive. She lost all her college photos, which is so sad because she studied abroad in Italy and had all these amazing pictures of the Colosseum and...\n\nOh, gosh. I'm sorry. I'm doing it again. What did you ask me? About being a kid? But yeah, no, I don't think I was unusually distracted back then.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about many different parts of your life, such as work, finances, health, or safety?\n\nPatient: Um, worrying excessively? I mean, I guess I worry a normal amount about work and stuff, but not excessively. I definitely check things a lot, but I wouldn't say I'm sitting around worrying about my health or finances all day. \n\nAlthough, speaking of finances, I did just have to pay my car insurance renewal, and it went up by like thirty dollars a month for absolutely no reason. I haven't even had a ticket or an accident or anything! I tried calling my agent, but he was out of the office for a golf tournament. It's crazy how much business gets done on golf courses, honestly. My boss plays golf every Friday afternoon with the VP of Sales, and they're always coming back with these huge new initiatives that the rest of us have to figure out how to implement by Monday. It's exhausting. I tried taking up golf once, just to see if I could network a bit better, but I'm so uncoordinated. I bought this starter set of clubs from a guy on Facebook Marketplace, but the bag smelled like old cigarettes. It's so hard to get smoke smell out of fabric. I tried using that Febreze stuff, but it just made it smell like fake lavender and smoke, which is almost worse. You really need, like, an ozone machine for that kind of thing. Hotels use them all the time when someone smokes in a non-smoking room...\n\nWait, sorry. I completely lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? About worrying? Yeah, no, I don't think I worry about all those different things excessively. Just the normal amount, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you try to stop pacing or checking the peephole, do you feel compelled to continue until it feels just right?\n\nPatient: Um, compelled to continue? I don't know if it's about it feeling \"just right,\" but... yeah, there are times I realize I’ve been pacing the same path from the kitchen to the front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers and checking the peephole over and over, even though nothing has actually happened. \n\nIt's just, the layout of my apartment makes it really easy to pace that exact route. It's an open-concept floor plan, which everyone says they want on those HGTV shows. Have you ever watched House Hunters? It's wild to me how people will have a budget of like, two million dollars, and their job is something like \"part-time hamster groomer.\" But anyway, open concept is great for light, but terrible for cooking smells. Whenever I make salmon, the whole place smells like fish for two days. I tried lighting one of those expensive Bath & Body Works candles—the mahogany teakwood one, which smells amazing, kind of like men's cologne?—but it barely made a dent. I think you're supposed to simmer lemons and rosemary on the stove to really get rid of cooking odors. My grandma used to do that. She had this huge rosemary bush in her backyard that practically took over the whole fence. Rosemary is so resilient, you barely even have to water it. I tried growing a basil plant on my windowsill once, but I forgot to water it for three days and it completely shriveled up. I just don't have a green thumb at all, which is sad because I'd love to have one of those trendy fiddle leaf figs...\n\nWait, I'm doing it again, aren't I? I'm so sorry. What did you ask me? About the peephole? But yeah, I just... I do keep pacing and checking it, and rubbing my fingers together, even when I know nobody is there.\n\nDoctor: Did you regularly lose your train of thought and jump between topics like this during childhood?\n\nPatient: Um, losing my train of thought like this when I was a kid? No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel like this is a newer thing for me. When I was younger, I was pretty focused, especially when I was reading. I used to read those Animorphs books all the time. Do you remember those? The covers always had that weird lenticular thing where the kid would slowly turn into a hawk or a dolphin or whatever. I had the entire collection lined up on my bookshelf in rainbow order. I'm still kind of obsessed with color-coding things. My closet is completely color-coordinated, from white all the way to black. It makes getting dressed in the morning so much easier, especially because I have to wear business casual to the office. Finding good business casual pants that aren't incredibly stiff and uncomfortable is practically impossible. I usually just end up buying the exact same pair of black slacks from Ann Taylor Loft over and over again whenever they have a sale. They send out so many promotional emails, though. I swear I get three emails a day from them. I really need to sit down and unsubscribe from all those mailing lists. My inbox has like, forty thousand unread emails in it right now. It gives my coworker hives just looking at my screen...\n\nOh, I'm sorry. I did it again. What was the question? About being a kid? Yeah, no, I definitely didn't jump around like this back then. I was pretty focused.\n\nDoctor: About how much time each day do you spend pacing or checking the peephole?\n\nPatient: Um, how much time? I don't know exactly. It's hard to keep track of time when I get like that. Time is so weird lately anyway. Have you noticed how fast this year is going? It's already almost October. I walked into Target yesterday to get paper towels, and they already had all the Christmas stuff out. It's ridiculous. They haven't even gotten through Halloween yet! I love Halloween, mostly for the candy. Those little fun-size Snickers bars are my weakness. I try to buy them at the last minute so I don't eat the whole bag before the trick-or-treaters come. Last year, I bought three bags of Reese's peanut butter cups in September, and I had to go back and buy more the day before Halloween because I ate them all while watching Netflix. I was watching that baking show, the Great British Baking Show. It's so soothing, you know? The music is so calm, and everyone is so polite to each other, even when their cakes collapse. I tried baking a cake once, a lemon drizzle one like they make on the show, but I forgot the baking powder and it came out like a dense, lemony brick. My dog wouldn't even eat it, and he eats everything. He ate a whole tube of chapstick once and was totally fine. It was the cherry flavored kind, too...\n\nWait, sorry. I'm doing it again. What did you ask? Oh, how much time I spend doing the pacing and checking? I guess... maybe a few hours a day? It just kind of takes over, and I don't realize how long I've been doing it.\n\nDoctor: When you try to stop pacing or checking, do you feel unable to resist the urge to keep doing it?\n\nPatient: Um, unable to resist the urge? I don't know if it's an urge, really. It's more like... I just find myself doing it. Like I mentioned, I'll just suddenly realize I've been pacing that same exact path, you know, kitchen to front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers, and looking through the peephole over and over, even when absolutely nothing has happened out there. \n\nIt's just so automatic. Kind of like driving to work, you know? Have you ever had that happen where you drive your whole commute and pull into the parking lot, and you don't even remember the drive? Highway hypnosis, I think they call it. My commute is awful, honestly. The traffic on I-95 is a nightmare no matter what time you leave. I tried leaving at 6:30 AM once to beat the rush, and there was still a bumper-to-bumper backup because a truck spilled a load of gravel across three lanes. I ended up listening to an entire true crime podcast episode while just sitting there. I love true crime podcasts, but sometimes they freak me out, especially the ones about unsolved mysteries. I always end up double-checking all my locks after I listen to them. I even bought one of those extra security bars for my sliding glass door, the kind you wedge into the track? My dad told me to get one when I moved into this apartment. He's always been really big on home security. He has cameras set up all over his house that send alerts to his phone every time a squirrel runs across the porch. He showed me the app once, and it was just dozens of notifications of a fat squirrel stealing birdseed...\n\nOh, gosh, I'm doing it again. I'm sorry. The urge to stop? I realize I'm already doing it, pacing and checking and rubbing my fingers, before I even think about stopping.\n\nDoctor: What feeling do you experience when you stop pacing or checking before you feel finished?\n\nPatient: Um, how do I feel if I stop before I'm finished? I don't really know if there is a \"finished\" feeling, honestly. It totally reminds me of those candy cigarettes we used to buy from the ice cream truck when I was a kid. Can you believe they actually sold those to children? We used to pretend we were smoking them behind the bleachers at recess. The ice cream man also had those SpongeBob popsicles where the gumball eyes were always melted and terrifying looking. I always wanted the Choco Taco, though. I was so sad when they discontinued those recently. I saw a petition online to bring them back, and it had like a hundred thousand signatures. People get really passionate about nostalgic snacks...\n\nOh, gosh, sorry. I went off again. What were we talking about? The pacing? Right, yeah. I don't really feel a specific way if I stop, because I don't really decide to start or stop.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi... well, I guess I came in because I just can’t stop feeling like everything that goes wrong is my fault. Like I’m a terrible employee, and a really bad homeowner, and I just deserve to lose everything I’ve worked for. \n\nI mean, I work in business operations, so I’m supposed to keep things running smoothly, right? But the other day, I was looking at my house and the gutters were completely clogged. I bought this ladder from Home Depot last month—it was one of those telescoping ones, they had a really good sale going on in the garden center. I actually went in there just to buy some potting soil for my ferns. Ferns are just so hard to keep alive indoors, the air gets way too dry in the winter. I tried misting them with a spray bottle, but it leaves these little water spots on the hardwood floors, and then you have to use that special wood cleaner, the orange-scented kind... \n\nWait, I'm sorry, what was I saying? I just... I look around and I just feel like I ruin everything I touch.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing repetitive behaviors or rituals to reduce anxiety or prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Um, rituals... I don't know if it's a ritual exactly, but there are times I realize I’ve just been pacing. Like, the exact same path from the kitchen to the front door, over and over. And I'll be rubbing my thumb against my fingers, just... checking the peephole repeatedly, even though nothing has actually happened. \n\nThe peephole is actually really hard to see through right now anyway because the glass got smudged. I tried cleaning it with Windex, but Windex always leaves streaks if you don't use a newspaper to wipe it. My mom always used to save the Sunday papers just for washing windows. Nobody really gets the physical newspaper delivered anymore, though, do they? It's all digital now. I tried subscribing to the digital Times a while back to stay on top of market trends for work, but my inbox just gets so cluttered with their daily briefing emails, and I hate having unread notifications on my phone. It drains the battery so fast when the screen keeps lighting up, and replacing an iPhone battery is practically the cost of a new phone these days... \n\nI'm sorry, I completely lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? But yeah, I just pace and check the door a lot for no real reason.\n\nDoctor: Before you repeatedly check the peephole or pace, do you experience an intrusive, unwanted fear or thought that these actions are meant to prevent or relieve?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think there's a specific thought like that. I'm not trying to prevent a disaster or anything, I just... I don't know, it's just this restless, awful feeling. Sort of like when you're waiting for a really important email and you just keep refreshing the page over and over. \n\nWe use Outlook at my office, and honestly, the servers have been so slow lately. IT did this huge migration to the cloud last week, and half the company was locked out of their accounts for hours. My manager was totally panicking because we had our quarter-end reports due, and I'm the one who usually compiles the data. I tried explaining to her that the cloud is supposed to be better for security anyway, especially with all those phishing scams going around right now. My aunt actually fell for one of those text message scams recently, the ones where they pretend to be the post office saying a package couldn't be delivered? She clicked the link and ended up having to cancel all her credit cards. Calling the bank to cancel a card is such a nightmare, you have to sit on hold listening to that awful elevator music for twenty minutes, and they always try to upsell you on fraud protection... \n\nUm. Sorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? I forgot what I was answering. But no, I don't have a specific fear before I check the door. I just do it.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt sad, empty, or down most of the day on most days?\n\nPatient: Sad or empty? Um, I don't think so. I haven't really kept track of my days like that, to be honest. \n\nI actually bought a new planner recently to try and be better about tracking my time, because in business ops you basically live and die by your calendar. It's one of those Moleskine ones with the dotted grids? They're supposed to be for bullet journaling. I tried getting into bullet journaling last year because I saw all these amazing, aesthetic spreads on Pinterest. Pinterest is so addictive, honestly. I spent like three hours the other night just pinning recipes for overnight oats. Oats are supposedly really good for your cholesterol, which I guess I shouldn't worry about too much at 27, but my dad just had to go on statins and he was complaining about the side effects. He said it makes his muscles ache, especially when he walks the dog. They have a golden retriever, she's so cute but she sheds absolutely everywhere. You really need a good vacuum for pet hair, like one of those cordless Dysons. Dysons are so expensive though, you really have to wait for the Black Friday sales if you want to get a decent deal on one... \n\nWait, I'm so sorry, I totally lost track again. What was the question? Oh, feeling down. Easily distracted... I don't really remember anyone saying that specifically. Michael's always has those 40% off coupons, but you have to download their app to use them now. My boss plays golf every Friday afternoon with the VP of Sales, and they're always coming back with these huge new initiatives that the rest of us have to figure out how to implement by Monday. It's exhausting. I tried taking up golf once, just to see if I could network a bit better, but I'm so uncoordinated. I bought this starter set of clubs from a guy on Facebook Marketplace, but the bag smelled like old cigarettes. It's so hard to get smoke smell out of fabric. I tried using that Febreze stuff, but it just made it smell like fake lavender and smoke, which is almost worse. You really need, like, an ozone machine for that kind of thing. Hotels use them all the time when someone smokes in a non-smoking room...\n\nWait, sorry. I completely lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? About worrying? Yeah, no, I don't think I worry about all those different things excessively. Just the normal amount, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you try to stop pacing or checking the peephole, do you feel compelled to continue until it feels just right?\n\nPatient: Um, compelled to continue? I don't know if it's about it feeling \"just right,\" but... yeah, there are times I realize I’ve been pacing the same path from the kitchen to the front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers and checking the peephole over and over, even though nothing has actually happened. \n\nIt's just, the layout of my apartment makes it really easy to pace that exact route. It's an open-concept floor plan, which everyone says they want on those HGTV shows. Have you ever watched House Hunters? It's wild to me how people will have a budget of like, two million dollars, and their job is something like \"part-time hamster groomer.\" But anyway, open concept is great for light, but terrible for cooking smells. Whenever I make salmon, the whole place smells like fish for two days. I tried lighting one of those expensive Bath & Body Works candles—the mahogany teakwood one, which smells amazing, kind of like men's cologne?—but it barely made a dent. I think you're supposed to simmer lemons and rosemary on the stove to really get rid of cooking odors. My grandma used to do that. She had this huge rosemary bush in her backyard that practically took over the whole fence. Rosemary is so resilient, you barely even have to water it. I tried growing a basil plant on my windowsill once, but I forgot to water it for three days and it completely shriveled up. I just don't have a green thumb at all, which is sad because I'd love to have one of those trendy fiddle leaf figs...\n\nWait, I'm doing it again, aren't I? I'm so sorry. What did you ask me? About the peephole? But yeah, I just... I do keep pacing and checking it, and rubbing my fingers together, even when I know nobody is there.\n\nDoctor: Did you regularly lose your train of thought and jump between topics like this during childhood?\n\nPatient: Um, losing my train of thought like this when I was a kid? No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel like this is a newer thing for me. When I was younger, I was pretty focused, especially when I was reading. I used to read those Animorphs books all the time. Do you remember those? The covers always had that weird lenticular thing where the kid would slowly turn into a hawk or a dolphin or whatever. I had the entire collection lined up on my bookshelf in rainbow order. I'm still kind of obsessed with color-coding things. My closet is completely color-coordinated, from white all the way to black. It makes getting dressed in the morning so much easier, especially because I have to wear business casual to the office. Finding good business casual pants that aren't incredibly stiff and uncomfortable is practically impossible. I usually just end up buying the exact same pair of black slacks from Ann Taylor Loft over and over again whenever they have a sale. They send out so many promotional emails, though. I swear I get three emails a day from them. I really need to sit down and unsubscribe from all those mailing lists. My inbox has like, forty thousand unread emails in it right now. It's hard to keep track of time when I get like that. I walked into Target yesterday to get paper towels, and they already had all the Christmas stuff out. It's ridiculous. They haven't even gotten through Halloween yet! I love Halloween, mostly for the candy. Those little fun-size Snickers bars are my weakness. I try to buy them at the last minute so I don't eat the whole bag before the trick-or-treaters come. Last year, I bought three bags of Reese's peanut butter cups in September, and I had to go back and buy more the day before Halloween because I ate them all while watching Netflix. I was watching that baking show, the Great British Baking Show. It's so soothing, you know? The music is so calm, and everyone is so polite to each other, even when their cakes collapse. I tried baking a cake once, a lemon drizzle one like they make on the show, but I forgot the baking powder and it came out like a dense, lemony brick. My dog wouldn't even eat it, and he eats everything. He ate a whole tube of chapstick once and was totally fine. It was the cherry flavored kind, too...\n\nWait, sorry. I'm doing it again. What did you ask? Oh, how much time I spend doing the pacing and checking? I guess... maybe a few hours a day? I don't know if it's an urge, really. It's more like... I just find myself doing it. Kind of like driving to work, you know? Have you ever had that happen where you drive your whole commute and pull into the parking lot, and you don't even remember the drive? I tried leaving at 6:30 AM once to beat the rush, and there was still a bumper-to-bumper backup because a truck spilled a load of gravel across three lanes. I ended up listening to an entire true crime podcast episode while just sitting there. I love true crime podcasts, but sometimes they freak me out, especially the ones about unsolved mysteries. I always end up double-checking all my locks after I listen to them. I even bought one of those extra security bars for my sliding glass door, the kind you wedge into the track? My dad told me to get one when I moved into this apartment. He's always been really big on home security. He has cameras set up all over his house that send alerts to his phone every time a squirrel runs across the porch. It gives me terrible heartburn. I had to start keeping a giant bottle of Tums in my bottom desk drawer. The berry flavored ones aren't too bad, but the chalky texture is just gross. It totally reminds me of those candy cigarettes we used to buy from the ice cream truck when I was a kid. Can you believe they actually sold those to children? We used to pretend we were smoking them behind the bleachers at recess. The ice cream man also had those SpongeBob popsicles where the gumball eyes were always melted and terrifying looking. I always wanted the Choco Taco, though. I was so sad when they discontinued those recently. I saw a petition online to bring them back, and it had like a hundred thousand signatures. People get really passionate about nostalgic snacks...\n\nOh, gosh, sorry. I went off again. What were we talking about? The pacing? Right, yeah. I don't really feel a specific way if I stop, because I don't really decide to start or stop. I just suddenly realize I've been doing the whole kitchen-to-door pacing and checking thing over and over for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do the pacing and repeated checking interfere with your daily life, such as your work, sleep, or ability to leave home?\n\nPatient: Um, interfere? You asked about work and leaving home. Right. So, yeah, I've made enough mistakes at work lately that my supervisor actually started checking in on my performance. I haven't even had a ticket or an accident or anything! I tried calling my agent, but he was out of the office for a golf tournament. It's crazy how much business gets done on golf courses, honestly. My boss plays golf every Friday afternoon with the VP of Sales, and they're always coming back with these huge new initiatives that the rest of us have to figure out how to implement by Monday. It's exhausting. I tried taking up golf once, just to see if I could network a bit better, but I'm so uncoordinated. I bought this starter set of clubs from a guy on Facebook Marketplace, but the bag smelled like old cigarettes. It's so hard to get smoke smell out of fabric. I tried using that Febreze stuff, but it just made it smell like fake lavender and smoke, which is almost worse. You really need, like, an ozone machine for that kind of thing. Hotels use them all the time when someone smokes in a non-smoking room...\n\nWait, sorry. I completely lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? About worrying? Yeah, no, I don't think I worry about all those different things excessively. Just the normal amount, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you try to stop pacing or checking the peephole, do you feel compelled to continue until it feels just right?\n\nPatient: Um, compelled to continue? I don't know if it's about it feeling \"just right,\" but... yeah, there are times I realize I’ve been pacing the same path from the kitchen to the front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers and checking the peephole over and over, even though nothing has actually happened. \n\nIt's just, the layout of my apartment makes it really easy to pace that exact route. It's an open-concept floor plan, which everyone says they want on those HGTV shows. Have you ever watched House Hunters? It's wild to me how people will have a budget of like, two million dollars, and their job is something like \"part-time hamster groomer.\" But anyway, open concept is great for light, but terrible for cooking smells. Whenever I make salmon, the whole place smells like fish for two days. I tried lighting one of those expensive Bath & Body Works candles—the mahogany teakwood one, which smells amazing, kind of like men's cologne?—but it barely made a dent. I think you're supposed to simmer lemons and rosemary on the stove to really get rid of cooking odors. My grandma used to do that. She had this huge rosemary bush in her backyard that practically took over the whole fence. Rosemary is so resilient, you barely even have to water it. I tried growing a basil plant on my windowsill once, but I forgot to water it for three days and it completely shriveled up. I just don't have a green thumb at all, which is sad because I'd love to have one of those trendy fiddle leaf figs...\n\nWait, I'm doing it again, aren't I? I'm so sorry. What did you ask me? About the peephole? But yeah, I just... I do keep pacing and checking it, and rubbing my fingers together, even when I know nobody is there.\n\nDoctor: Did you regularly lose your train of thought and jump between topics like this during childhood?\n\nPatient: Um, losing my train of thought like this when I was a kid? No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel like this is a newer thing for me. When I was younger, I was pretty focused, especially when I was reading. I used to read those Animorphs books all the time. Do you remember those? The covers always had that weird lenticular thing where the kid would slowly turn into a hawk or a dolphin or whatever. I had the entire collection lined up on my bookshelf in rainbow order. I'm still kind of obsessed with color-coding things. My closet is completely color-coordinated, from white all the way to black. Um, persistently anxious or worried? I don't really think so. I mean, I'm not sitting around worrying all day. I just feel kind of... flat, mostly. Or just tired. So tired. It's like that feeling when you stay up way too late watching a movie and the next day you just can't get your brain to wake up. I watched this documentary the other night about deep-sea creatures, and it was fascinating but also kind of terrifying. There are these fish that live so far down in the ocean that they create their own light, like bioluminescence. It's like they have little glow sticks built into their heads. Glow sticks remind me of Halloween when I was a kid. My mom used to crack them and put them inside balloons to make these glowing ghost decorations for our front porch. We had this huge oak tree in the front yard, and she would hang them from the branches. It looked so cool, until the wind blew too hard and popped half of them. 

Then we had to spend the whole next morning picking up little pieces of rubber from the grass so the lawnmower wouldn't chop them up. My dad was obsessed with his lawn. He used to measure the grass with a ruler to make sure he was cutting it at the exact right height. He had this special fertilizer schedule that he kept on a whiteboard in the garage. The garage always smelled like gasoline and cut grass, which is actually one of my favorite smells. That and the smell of rain right before a thunderstorm. 

Oh, sorry. I'm drifting again, aren't I? Um, you asked if I feel anxious when I'm not pacing. No, not really. I don't feel worried, just... yeah, mostly just tired and out of it.

Nobody really gets the physical newspaper delivered anymore, though, do they? It's all digital now. It's wild to me how people will have a budget of like, two million dollars, and their job is something like \"part-time hamster groomer.\" But anyway, open concept is great for light, but terrible for cooking smells. Whenever I make salmon, the whole place smells like fish for two days. I tried lighting one of those expensive Bath & Body Works candles—the mahogany teakwood one, which smells amazing, kind of like men's cologne?—but it barely made a dent. I think you're supposed to simmer lemons and rosemary on the stove to really get rid of cooking odors. My grandma used to do that. She had this huge rosemary bush in her backyard that practically took over the whole fence. Rosemary is so resilient, you barely even have to water it. I tried growing a basil plant on my windowsill once, but I forgot to water it for three days and it completely shriveled up. I just don't have a green thumb at all, which is sad because I'd love to have one of those trendy fiddle leaf figs...\n\nWait, I'm doing it again, aren't I? I'm so sorry. What did you ask me? About the peephole? But yeah, I just... I do keep pacing and checking it, and rubbing my fingers together, even when I know nobody is there.\n\nDoctor: Did you regularly lose your train of thought and jump between topics like this during childhood?\n\nPatient: Um, losing my train of thought like this when I was a kid? No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel like this is a newer thing for me. When I was younger, I was pretty focused, especially when I was reading. I used to read those Animorphs books all the time. Do you remember those? The covers always had that weird lenticular thing where the kid would slowly turn into a hawk or a dolphin or whatever. I had the entire collection lined up on my bookshelf in rainbow order. I'm still kind of obsessed with color-coding things. My closet is completely color-coordinated, from white all the way to black. It makes getting dressed in the morning so much easier, especially because I have to wear business casual to the office. Finding good business casual pants that aren't incredibly stiff and uncomfortable is practically impossible. I usually just end up buying the exact same pair of black slacks from Ann Taylor Loft over and over again whenever they have a sale. They send out so many promotional emails, though. I swear I get three emails a day from them. I really need to sit down and unsubscribe from all those mailing lists. My inbox has like, forty thousand unread emails in it right now. It gives my coworker hives just looking at my screen...\n\nOh, I'm sorry. I did it again. What was the question? About being a kid? Yeah, no, I definitely didn't jump around like this back then. I was pretty focused.\n\nDoctor: About how much time each day do you spend pacing or checking the peephole?\n\nPatient: Um, how much time? I don't know exactly. It's hard to keep track of time when I get like that. But like I said before, I just realize I've been pacing that same path, kitchen to front door, rubbing my thumb on my fingers, checking the peephole over and over, even when nothing's happened. \n\nIt feels like it can go on for a while, though. Time is so weird lately anyway. Have you noticed how fast this year is going? It's already almost October. I walked into Target yesterday to get paper towels, and they already had all the Christmas stuff out. It's ridiculous. They haven't even gotten through Halloween yet! I love Halloween, mostly for the candy. Those little fun-size Snickers bars are my weakness. I try to buy them at the last minute so I don't eat the whole bag before the trick-or-treaters come. Last year, I bought three bags of Reese's peanut butter cups in September, and I had to go back and buy more the day before Halloween because I ate them all while watching Netflix. I was watching that baking show, the Great British Baking Show. It's so soothing, you know? The music is so calm, and everyone is so polite to each other, even when their cakes collapse. I tried baking a cake once, a lemon drizzle one like they make on the show, but I forgot the baking powder and it came out like a dense, lemony brick. My dog wouldn't even eat it, and he eats everything. He ate a whole tube of chapstick once and was totally fine. It was the cherry flavored kind, too...\n\nWait, sorry. I'm doing it again. What did you ask? Oh, how much time I spend doing the pacing and checking? I guess... maybe a few hours a day? It just kind of takes over, and I don't realize how long I've been doing it.\n\nDoctor: When you try to stop pacing or checking, do you feel unable to resist the urge to keep doing it?\n\nPatient: Um, unable to resist the urge? I don't know if it's an urge, really. It's more like... I just find myself doing it. Like I mentioned, I'll just suddenly realize I've been pacing that same exact path, you know, kitchen to front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers, and looking through the peephole over and over, even when absolutely nothing has happened out there. \n\nIt's just so automatic. Kind of like driving to work, you know? Have you ever had that happen where you drive your whole commute and pull into the parking lot, and you don't even remember the drive? Highway hypnosis, I think they call it. My commute is awful, honestly. The traffic on I-95 is a nightmare no matter what time you leave. I tried leaving at 6:30 AM once to beat the rush, and there was still a bumper-to-bumper backup because a truck spilled a load of gravel across three lanes. I ended up listening to an entire true crime podcast episode while just sitting there. I love true crime podcasts, but sometimes they freak me out, especially the ones about unsolved mysteries. I always end up double-checking all my locks after I listen to them. I even bought one of those extra security bars for my sliding glass door, the kind you wedge into the track? My dad told me to get one when I moved into this apartment. He's always been really big on home security. He has cameras set up all over his house that send alerts to his phone every time a squirrel runs across the porch. He showed me the app once, and it was just dozens of notifications of a fat squirrel stealing birdseed...\n\nOh, gosh, I'm doing it again. I'm sorry. The urge to stop? Yeah, I don't know if it's an urge I can't resist, it's just... I realize I'm already doing it, pacing and checking and rubbing my fingers, before I even think about stopping.\n\nDoctor: What feeling do you experience when you stop pacing or checking before you feel finished?\n\nPatient: Um, how do I feel if I stop before I'm finished? I don't really know if there is a \"finished\" feeling, honestly. Like I was saying a minute ago, there are just times I realize I’ve been pacing that same exact path from the kitchen to the front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers and checking the peephole over and over. And nothing has actually happened out there to make me do it. So if I try to just force myself to sit down, I guess I just feel kind of... jittery? \n\nLike I have all this nervous energy, kind of like when you drink way too much caffeine. Our office actually just got one of those fancy new espresso machines in the breakroom. It's supposed to be this great employee perk, but honestly, the beans they buy for it are so acidic. It gives me terrible heartburn. I had to start keeping a giant bottle of Tums in my bottom desk drawer. The berry flavored ones aren't too bad, but the chalky texture is just gross. It totally reminds me of those candy cigarettes we used to buy from the ice cream truck when I was a kid. Can you believe they actually sold those to children? We used to pretend we were smoking them behind the bleachers at recess. The ice cream man also had those SpongeBob popsicles where the gumball eyes were always melted and terrifying looking. I always wanted the Choco Taco, though. I was so sad when they discontinued those recently. I saw a petition online to bring them back, and it had like a hundred thousand signatures. People get really passionate about nostalgic snacks...\n\nOh, gosh, sorry. I went off again. What were we talking about? The pacing? Right, yeah. I don't really feel a specific way if I stop, because I don't really decide to start or stop. I just suddenly realize I've been doing the whole kitchen-to-door pacing and checking thing over and over for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do the pacing and repeated checking interfere with your daily life, such as your work, sleep, or ability to leave home?\n\nPatient: Um, interfere? Yeah, I guess it really does. I mean, I've missed some social plans lately because of it, and I've let the townhouse get way messier than I can usually stand. Speaking of messy, I keep meaning to buy one of those robot vacuums to help out. My friend Sarah has one, she named it Dustin, which I think is just hilarious. She put googly eyes on it and everything. It just bumps into the baseboards and her cat swats at it. I would love a cat, but my lease doesn't let me have one. The pet deposit is like five hundred dollars anyway, which is insane just to have a tiny animal sleep on your sofa all day. Sofas are so expensive now, too. I got mine at Ikea and building it took me like four hours, and I still had three screws left over. I put them in a kitchen drawer, and I swear that drawer is just a graveyard for random things. Batteries, soy sauce packets, rubber bands, takeout menus for places that probably closed five years ago...\n\nOh, gosh, sorry. I'm doing the rambling thing again. You asked about work and leaving home. Right. So, yeah, I've made enough mistakes at work lately that my supervisor actually started checking in on my performance. I am still working, but it just takes so much more effort to keep up with basic responsibilities right now. It's really draining.\n\nDoctor: Do you have recurring intrusive thoughts, images, or urges that keep coming into your mind and feel difficult to dismiss?\n\nPatient: Um, intrusive thoughts? Images? No, I don't think so. I mean, my mind wanders a lot, obviously, but it's not like... bad thoughts or urges that I can't get rid of. It's just normal stuff, you know? Like remembering I need to buy milk, or wondering if I left the straightener plugged in. I actually did leave it plugged in once in college, and it scorched the bathroom counter in my dorm. My RA was so mad. She was this super intense senior who treated the job like she was running a maximum-security prison. It took like three days for the paint to dry, and it smelled awful, like vinegar and plastic. We sat all the way up in the nosebleed section, but we screamed so loud I lost my voice for a week. \n\nOh, right, sorry. I'm doing it again. Um, but no, to answer your question, I don't have any weird urges or images stuck in my head. It was super easy, except I dropped one of the plastic clips into the glovebox hinge and it took me twenty minutes to fish it out with a pair of tweezers. Tweezers are so useful, though. I buy the really sharp ones for my eyebrows, but I always end up using them for random stuff, like getting splinters out or untying really tight knots in my shoelaces. I bought these new running shoes last month because I told myself I was going to start jogging in the mornings. They're this really bright neon coral color. I've only worn them once, to walk to the mailbox. \n\nOh, gosh. Sorry. I totally lost track of the question again. Did I even answer it? You were asking if pacing makes me feel less anxious. Yeah, I don't really think it changes how I feel. I don't really notice a difference before or after.\n\nDoctor: When you are not pacing or checking the peephole, do you still feel persistently anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: Um, persistently anxious or worried? I don't really think so. I mean, I'm not sitting around worrying all day. I just feel kind of... flat, mostly. Or just tired. So tired. I watched this documentary the other night about deep-sea creatures, and it was fascinating but also kind of terrifying. Glow sticks remind me of Halloween when I was a kid. My mom used to crack them and put them inside balloons to make these glowing ghost decorations for our front porch. People get really passionate about nostalgic snacks...\n\nOh, gosh, sorry. I went off again. What were we talking about? The pacing? Right, yeah. I don't really feel a specific way if I stop, because I don't really decide to start or stop. I just suddenly realize I've been doing the whole kitchen-to-door pacing and checking thing over and over for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do the pacing and repeated checking interfere with your daily life, such as your work, sleep, or ability to leave home?\n\nPatient: Um, interfere? Yeah, I guess it really does. I mean, I've missed some social plans lately because of it, and I've let the townhouse get way messier than I can usually stand. Speaking of messy, I keep meaning to buy one of those robot vacuums to help out. My friend Sarah has one, she named it Dustin, which I think is just hilarious. She put googly eyes on it and everything. It just bumps into the baseboards and her cat swats at it. I would love a cat, but my lease doesn't let me have one. The pet deposit is like five hundred dollars anyway, which is insane just to have a tiny animal sleep on your sofa all day. Sofas are so expensive now, too. I got mine at Ikea and building it took me like four hours, and I still had three screws left over. I put them in a kitchen drawer, and I swear that drawer is just a graveyard for random things. Batteries, soy sauce packets, rubber bands, takeout menus for places that probably closed five years ago...\n\nOh, gosh, sorry. I'm doing the rambling thing again. You asked about work and leaving home. Right. So, yeah, I've made enough mistakes at work lately that my supervisor actually started checking in on my performance. I am still working, but it just takes so much more effort to keep up with basic responsibilities right now. It's really draining.\n\nDoctor: Do you have recurring intrusive thoughts, images, or urges that keep coming into your mind and feel difficult to dismiss?\n\nPatient: Um, intrusive thoughts? Images? No, I don't think so. I mean, my mind wanders a lot, obviously, but it's not like... bad thoughts or urges that I can't get rid of. It's just normal stuff, you know? Like remembering I need to buy milk, or wondering if I left the straightener plugged in. I actually did leave it plugged in once in college, and it scorched the bathroom counter in my dorm. My RA was so mad. She was this super intense senior who treated the job like she was running a maximum-security prison. She used to do room checks with a literal clipboard and write us up for having too many posters on the walls because it was a \"fire hazard.\" I mean, the whole building was cinderblock, it wasn't going to burn down from a Jonas Brothers poster. I went to a concert of theirs back in 2008, actually. My mom drove me and my best friend, and we made these puffy paint t-shirts that said \"Mrs. Jonas\" on them. It took like three days for the paint to dry, and it smelled awful, like vinegar and plastic. We sat all the way up in the nosebleed section, but we screamed so loud I lost my voice for a week. \n\nOh, right, sorry. I'm doing it again. Um, but no, to answer your question, I don't have any weird urges or images stuck in my head. Just regular thoughts, really.\n\nDoctor: After you pace or check the peephole, does your nervous or anxious feeling temporarily decrease?\n\nPatient: Um, does it decrease the nervous feeling? I'm not really sure if it does, honestly. It's not like I'm doing it to calm down, I just kind of find myself doing it. It's almost like muscle memory at this point, you know? Like when you drive home from work and suddenly you're pulling into your driveway and you don't even remember the last five miles. I hate when that happens. It's so scary to think your brain just goes on autopilot while you're operating a two-ton metal machine. \n\nI actually need to get my car's oil changed soon. The little sticker on the windshield says I'm like five hundred miles over. I usually go to this place down the street because they have free coffee in the waiting room, but last time the mechanic tried to tell me I needed a new cabin air filter and quoted me eighty bucks. I looked it up on YouTube while I was sitting there, and the filter was only like fifteen dollars at the auto parts store. I changed it myself in the parking lot. It was super easy, except I dropped one of the plastic clips into the glovebox hinge and it took me twenty minutes to fish it out with a pair of tweezers. Tweezers are so useful, though. I buy the really sharp ones for my eyebrows, but I always end up using them for random stuff, like getting splinters out or untying really tight knots in my shoelaces. I bought these new running shoes last month because I told myself I was going to start jogging in the mornings. They're this really bright neon coral color. I've only worn them once, to walk to the mailbox. \n\nOh, gosh. Sorry. I totally lost track of the question again. Did I even answer it? You were asking if pacing makes me feel less anxious. Yeah, I don't really think it changes how I feel. I don't really notice a difference before or after.\n\nDoctor: When you are not pacing or checking the peephole, do you still feel persistently anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: Um, persistently anxious or worried? I don't really think so. I mean, I'm not sitting around worrying all day. I just feel kind of... flat, mostly. Or just tired. So tired. It's like that feeling when you stay up way too late watching a movie and the next day you just can't get your brain to wake up. I watched this documentary the other night about deep-sea creatures, and it was fascinating but also kind of terrifying. There are these fish that live so far down in the ocean that they create their own light, like bioluminescence. It's like they have little glow sticks built into their heads. Glow sticks remind me of Halloween when I was a kid. My mom used to crack them and put them inside balloons to make these glowing ghost decorations for our front porch. We had this huge oak tree in the front yard, and she would hang them from the branches. It looked so cool, until the wind blew too hard and popped half of them. \n\nThen we had to spend the whole next morning picking up little pieces of rubber from the grass so the lawnmower wouldn't chop them up. My dad was obsessed with his lawn. He used to measure the grass with a ruler to make sure he was cutting it at the exact right height. That and the smell of rain right before a thunderstorm. \n\nOh, sorry. I'm drifting again, aren't I? Um, you asked if I feel anxious when I'm not pacing. No, not really. I don't feel worried, just... yeah, mostly just tired and out of it.\n\nDoctor: After one of these pacing and checking episodes ends, can you remember everything you did during it?\n\nPatient: Um, remember everything? Yeah, I think so. I mean, it's not like I black out or anything. I just walk back and forth and look out the door. It's pretty boring, really. Not like there's much to remember. It reminds me of this time I had to do inventory at work. I'm a business operations specialist, and we had this huge warehouse audit, and I literally spent eight hours just walking up and down the aisles counting boxes of printer paper and toner cartridges. It was mind-numbing. I tried listening to a podcast to pass the time, but the warehouse manager said it was a safety hazard to have headphones in. Which I guess makes sense because of the forklifts, but still. \n\nThe forklifts are actually kind of cool, though. One of the guys let me drive one once when the manager was on his lunch break. It's a lot harder than it looks, the steering is super sensitive. I almost backed into a pallet of sticky notes. Can you imagine? Just an avalanche of neon yellow squares everywhere. I use sticky notes for everything on my desk. I color-code them. Pink is for urgent stuff, blue is for emails I need to reply to, and green is for, like, personal reminders. I had to buy my own green ones because the office supply closet only ever has yellow. \n\nOh, right, the pacing. Yeah, I remember doing it. I don't forget it or anything. It's just... yeah, I remember.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you feel detached from yourself or your surroundings, as if you are observing yourself from outside or the world feels unreal?\n\nPatient: Um, detached? Like I'm watching myself from the outside? No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel like I'm right there in my body doing it. It's not like a movie or anything. Or like those security cameras we have at the office. \n\nThey actually just upgraded our whole security system last week because someone kept stealing lunches from the breakroom fridge. It was this huge drama. HR had to send out a mass email about respect and community property, and they even put a camera pointing right at the refrigerator door. I don't even use the fridge anyway because I bring my lunch in one of those insulated bags. I got it on Amazon and it has little penguins all over it. Penguins are honestly my favorite animal. I went to the zoo a few years ago and they had this penguin exhibit where you could watch them swim underwater through this massive glass wall. They look so clumsy when they waddle around on land, but in the water, they are basically little torpedoes. I took like a hundred pictures on my phone, but my phone completely ran out of storage right in the middle of it. I really need to back up my photos to the cloud, but I keep forgetting my Apple password. I used to use the exact same password for everything, which I know is terrible for cybersecurity, but then I had to change it for my bank account and now I'm just totally mixed up and get locked out of everything. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Um, stop myself? I guess if I really think about it, I can. But usually I don't even realize I'm doing it right away. Like, there are times I realize I’ve been pacing the exact same path from the kitchen to the front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers and checking the peephole over and over, even though nothing has actually happened. It's just this weird loop I get stuck in.

Oh, gosh, I'm doing it again. Sorry. Um, you asked if I can stop pacing. Yeah, if I catch myself doing the kitchen-to-door thing and rubbing my fingers, I can make myself sit down for a few minutes. It just takes a lot of effort to break the cycle once I realize I'm in it.
